**Synthesis (all three providers agree):** cost is now routinely reported; **wall-clock time and repeats are not**. Latency is the weakest link — AsyncLM emulates it, Speculate-with-Memory estimates it, AgentDiet avoids it, OSWorld 2.0 omits it; only OSWorld-Human and AI Agents That Matter treat time as a first-class Pareto axis. No surveyed acceleration paper pre-registers a non-inferiority margin, clusters standard errors by task, or reports pass^k for accelerated vs baseline agents. Price dates are rarely stated (Efficient Agents' "as of May 2025" is the good exception, but it omits the prices). Serving papers follow the same pattern: of 15 2026 agent-serving systems checked in followup-5, 12 report no task-quality number, none reports repeats, and none uses a GUI workload.

## 4.2 What "lossless" should mean (three senses, merged)

1. **Behavioral preservation by construction.** The authoritative model's decisions are unchanged; speculative results commit only on exact match of action type and all arguments; incorrect work cannot alter externally visible state (read-only whitelist, isolation, rollback, semantic guards; Atomix-style epochs and sealed transactions; Spork-style KV forking). State the assumptions: deterministic/replayable observations, cache freshness, resource contention, and that "lossless" means preserving the *baseline agent's behavior*, not task correctness. Examples: Speculative Actions, Speculate-with-Memory, PASTE (with audit), ISP's exact OpenAGI mode; ISP's fuzzy TravelPlanner mode explicitly loses the guarantee [O, C, G].
2. **Statistical non-inferiority.** Task success or field accuracy may differ, but a pre-specified one-sided confidence bound excludes degradation beyond δ. This is "non-inferior within δ points", not losslessness [O, C].
3. **No observed degradation.** Equal or slightly better point estimates ("comparable accuracy", "96.7% of OWL", "not statistically significant"). This establishes neither of the above [O, C].

Corollaries: a replay completing every action is not proof the requested fields are correct (PreAct documents 100% coverage with evaluator score zero); equal final answers do not prove equal policy compliance or absence of side effects; a web-agent answer key should check requested values **and** state invariants [O]. Gemini's stricter operational definition — final trajectory, state transitions and environmental side effects "mathematically, semantically and observably identical" — is sense 1 plus an invariants audit [G].

## 4.3 Recommended protocol for the first paper (unified)

**Design.** 25 held-out tasks spanning several workflow families and horizons, selected by a documented stratified procedure (not baseline successes); separate development tasks; split by workflow template to avoid near-duplicate leakage. 6 conditions (unchanged agent; simple batched-action baseline; full method; 3 pre-declared ablations) × 3 models × 3 repeats = **1,350 episodes**, ~100 model calls each. Pair every accelerated run with the baseline on the same task/model/repeat index; freeze tasks, evaluator, normalizers, margins and exclusion rules in a timestamped pre-registration [O, C].

**Primary metrics.** (1) Per-field accuracy: micro and macro F1 over normalized fields (exact identifiers; canonical dates/units; numeric tolerance; list-order rules); macro averages fields within a task then tasks so a 100-field form does not dominate a 5-field one; report missing / incorrect / extra / critical-field errors separately; LLM-judge with rubric trees only for free-text fields, with judge–human agreement (κ) on a sample [C, O, G]. (2) Strict task success: all required fields, all required actions committed, all invariants satisfied. (3) End-to-end wall-clock: request accepted → final committed state; mean, median, p90/p95 with uncertainty; **success-within-time curves** and a deadline-adjusted time (failures assigned the common deadline H) so fast failures cannot look like wins [O].

**Secondary metrics.** pass^1, pass^3 (all three pass), pass@3 (any), per-task outcome matrix; model calls split by role (actor/planner, draft, verifier, compressor, memory builder, retries) plus critical-path sequential depth; environment actions, tool RPCs, screenshots; tokens by class (input, cache read, cache write, visible output, billable reasoning, image) with raw provider usage saved for re-pricing; dollars for all attempts incl. failed branches and auxiliary models, list-price-normalized and actual; cost-of-pass as the pooled ratio (total $ / successes) **and** the taskwise average of C_i/p_i (flagged as unstable with 3 trials; undefined if zero successes); mechanism metrics (acceptance/cache-hit/fallback rates, wasted speculative work, call reduction, context growth, side-effect/invariant violations); optionally VAF and GDS [O, C, G].

**Lossless claims.** Tier 1 (construction): exact-match commit, read-only whitelist, logged commits/rollbacks, and a trajectory-equivalence audit with actor outputs held fixed (replay or cached temperature-0 responses) requiring byte-identical action sequences and final answers in 100% of runs. Tier 2 (outcome, live): H₀: F1_accel − F1_base ≤ −δ with δ pre-registered (Claude proposes 5 pp on field F1; ChatGPT proposes 2 pp on strict success and 1 pp on macro field accuracy, both gates required); task-level paired differences averaged over repeats; **task-clustered paired bootstrap ≥10,000 resamples** (never treat fields, steps or the 1,350 runs as independent); one-sided 95% lower bound > −δ; Holm correction across the ablations; TOST for two-sided equivalence; McNemar on success; report final-answer equivalence rate [C, O].

**Power and the limits of 25 tasks.** n ≈ ((z₀.₉₅ + z₀.₈₀)·σ_d/δ)² = ((1.645 + 0.84)·0.10/0.05)² ≈ 24.7 → 25 tasks give 80% power at δ = 5 pp only if the task-level paired SD ≤ 0.10 (estimate from a pilot) [C]. Zero observed regressions in 25 independent tasks still leaves a one-sided 95% binomial upper bound of 1 − 0.05^(1/25) = **11.3%** on the task-level regression rate; getting that below 2% would take ~149 independent tasks; repeats and correlated fields do not substitute for tasks; hand-picked tasks are not repaired by intervals [O]. Frame the result as "X× faster and Y% cheaper on this task distribution with estimated quality difference Δ in [L, U]" and reserve "lossless" for Tier 1.

**Confound control.** Replayable or self-hosted environments (WebArena-style Docker, record-and-replay proxy), state reset before every run, pinned browser; dated model snapshot IDs and run dates; interleave conditions in time; fix concurrency per condition, log 429s, report wall-clock with and without rate-limit back-off; one region; **one API tier** (never mix standard/fast/flex — OpenAI Fast mode is 2× price and 2.5× speed; Anthropic fast mode has its own rate limit); count speculator/verifier/compressor tokens and calls in cost even when hidden in time; separate cold-start, seen-workflow reuse and unseen-workflow transfer; start each repetition from the same skill/cache corpus; charge learning/compilation/verification; report C_build + N·C_run and the break-even N [C, O].

**Failure handling.** Identical deadlines; timeouts count as failures and keep their time/cost; report all-attempt and success-conditioned latency plus success-within-time curves; common-success subsets are post-treatment diagnostics only; predefine infrastructure-failure rerun rules, keep both records, report sensitivity [O].

**Artifacts.** Scorer code + tests, task manifests, model/provider/version, evaluation dates, reasoning settings, prompts, environment/container versions, task-order seeds, raw traces, usage ledger, aggregate analysis, and the **actual full-project spend** including failed development experiments, distinguishing API bills, hardware inventory, GPU-hours and human labor [O, C].

**Presentation.** Table 1: per model × condition — field F1 (±CI), success, pass^3, wall-clock median (IQR), speedup (geometric mean of per-pair ratios), calls, tokens (in/cached/out), $/run, cost-of-pass. Table 2: non-inferiority results (ΔF1, lower bound, δ, verdict) and trajectory-equivalence audit rates. Figures: cost–F1 and time–F1 Pareto plots with convex hulls per model; stacked per-step latency (LLM / env / hidden); for asynchronous execution, a timestamped critical-path trace rather than summed durations [C, O].

**Checklist mapping.** ABC (empty/default fields score 0; adversarial scorer tests); Miller (clustered SEs, paired differences, power, no temperature games); Kapoor et al. (cost-controlled comparison, simple strong baselines such as retry or a cheaper model alone, standardized logging) [C].

**Reconciled guidance.** At ~20K input / 400–500 output tokens per call, a frontier + mid + small trio costs **≈$4–7k uncached** or **≈$1.7–2.6k with ~80% cache reads**; an all-top-tier trio ≈$12–22k uncached; a budget trio <$2k. Sensitivities: every extra 1,000 billable output tokens per call adds ≈$920 (practical mix) to ≈$2,720 (Astra mix) [O]; Opus 5.5 at 2,000 output tokens/call adds ≈$1,440 per model [C]; the Claude 4.7+ tokenizer can add ≈30% to Anthropic rows [C]; speculative conditions bill the discarded branches (Gemini's assumed ~2× for four conditions; DSP measured +63% cost) [G, O]; a 30% call reduction in the five accelerated conditions lowers the total ≈25%, 50% lowers it ≈42% [C]. Reserve **~$5.5–7k** for a first study on the practical trio (20K/500, no cache, +30% for pilots, reruns, judge calls, VM/browser/search fees); ~$2.2k if a pilot confirms the cache mix; ~$16k if a GPT-6 Astra or Fable 5.1 arm is required [O, C]. Run a one-task pilot per model first to measure real per-call tokens, average and p95 calls, cache categories and reasoning output — hosted tokenizers and image accounting differ, and OpenAI's 25 Sep 2026 changelog notes an image-encoding fix affecting Sol/Luna computer-use evaluations, so pin evaluation dates [O].

## 4.6 Wall-clock and rate-limit planning

~10 s per step (LLM + browser) fits the published timings (OSWorld-Human 12 min for a trivial task; Speculative Actions' Table 1: 10–20 min OS tasks, 5–30 min deep research, 30–45 min data pipelines, 1 h chess). 100 steps ≈ 17 min per episode; 1,350 episodes ≈ 382 h sequential (≈16 days), ≈1.6 days at 10-way parallelism, ≈4 days if split by condition. At medium tokens, 10 concurrent runs at ~6 calls/min need ~60 requests/min and ~1.2M input tokens/min per provider — check tier limits; Anthropic fast mode returns 429 with retry-after on its own limit; Gemini has Standard/Flex/Priority tiers [C]. Log client-side request duration, TTFT if available, tool time, observation processing and rate-limit waiting; do not call these server-side prefill/decode unless the provider exposes them [O].

# PART V — Prompt 5: Novelty assessment of the SAP Concur acceleration plan

## 5.1 The plan as described in the prompts (user's own measurements)

Target: LLM agents filing expense reports in SAP Concur, a repetitive enterprise web workflow with a policy-derived, field-level answer key. Measured today: **~13 minutes and ~$3 per report; 71% of wall-clock spent waiting on the model; 57% of model calls execute a page-determined next step; many UI transitions have probability ≈1.0.** Proposed levers: (a) recipes compiled from the agent's own runs plus a few human demos, replayed deterministically with LLM fallback; (b) speculative execution of predictable UI transitions; (c) observation pruning, prefix caching, small-model routing; (d) accuracy–latency–cost Pareto frontiers over repeated runs with field-level accuracy loss attributed to each lever. Both novelty reports (Claude, Gemini) were given the same description; the two headline profile numbers (71% model wait, 57% page-determined) place the setting in Regime A of §1.8, which is where model-side levers have the most headroom.

## 5.2 Verdict (merged)

None of the four components is new on its own; a wave of April–September 2026 preprints sits very close to the plan (Agentic Compilation Apr, SkillDroid Apr, Skim May, Agent JIT May, PANDO May, MFS May, SpeedRunner Aug, SMC Sep, and — added in v2 — **EchoPath Sep 2026**, which already does guarded GUI-trajectory replay with preconditions and a (described, unevaluated) promote/repair/quarantine lifecycle on 159 OSWorld-Verified tasks with active memories [C15; verified 2026-09-27]). Two facts from doc 15 nonetheless support the plan's positioning: no paper reports speculative GUI actions on OSWorld or WebArena, and the Sep-2026 Efficient GUI Agents survey reportedly covers neither multi-action chunking nor speculative GUI execution [C15]. What is still open is narrower and defensible: (i) speculative execution of **state-changing UI transitions on a live SaaS portal that cannot be snapshotted**, with commit/compensation semantics; (ii) **profile-guided recipe compilation with guards and "de-optimize to LLM" fallback**, learned from own runs plus a few demos, on write-heavy multi-page forms, measuring the marginal value of each source; (iii) **field-level accounting of accuracy lost per acceleration lever**, over repeated runs, with latency as a third Pareto axis. Framed as "we combined known tricks on SAP Concur," it reads as an engineering report; framed around the three gaps, measured against the 2026 systems, and extended to public benchmarks, it is publishable [C]. Gemini reaches the same verdict with a stronger emphasis on the speculative-UI-execution component as "largely uncharted" and on "agentic JIT compilation" as a unifying narrative [G].

Gemini additionally cites, without numbers, FlowEvo, [FactorMiner](https://arxiv.org/html/2602.14670v1) (self-evolving agent with skills and experience, Feb 2026), [SkillEvolver](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2605.10500) (skill learning as a meta-skill, May 2026) and [Jev-Mem](https://arxiv.org/html/2609.23986v1) (System-One-controlled agentic memory, Sep 2026) as skill-library neighbours; treat them as leads for the related-work section, not as measured baselines.

## 5.4 Done vs open, component by component

**(a) Recipes from own runs + human demos, deterministic replay, LLM fallback — partially done, and narrower after v2.** Done: executable skills induced from own trajectories (ASI, SkillWeaver, SpeedRunner, SkillDroid, PANDO); deterministic replay with LLM healing, shipped in products (UiPath Healing Agent, Power Automate self-healing, Automation Anywhere Generative Recorder, Stagehand caching with a pre-replay DOM-fingerprint guard, Skyvern code caching, Hyperbrowser HyperAgent) and described in Agentic Compilation (healing not evaluated); tool-call record/replay with no replay-time fallback (LOOP) — none of them publishes a measured heal or replay success rate (follow-up 3, E160); site-profile fast paths with verifier and fallback (Skim); hidden mined macros committed at runtime (SMC); **guarded GUI-trajectory replay with preconditions, bounded parameters, visual re-aiming and a promote/repair/quarantine lifecycle on OSWorld-Verified (EchoPath, Sep 2026)** [C15]; API-first execution that removes the GUI steps altogether (AXIS, ComputerRL, UFO2). Still open: no work combines own-run mining **with** a few human demonstrations and measures each source's marginal value; none calibrates the fallback trigger against a field-level error budget; none evaluates on multi-page, write-heavy enterprise forms with policy constraints; **no work measures replay success across UI versions over time** (EchoPath is a paired repeat-execution setting on 159 first-pass-validated tasks, against a Synapse memory baseline, with only a screen-resolution shift between passes; its offline start-state gate rejects 78% of incompatible starts) [C15; verified 2026-09-27]; de-optimization semantics (what state the LLM resumes from when a guard fails mid-recipe) are underexplored — Skim's warm start at the fast path's final URL and EchoPath's repair state are the closest [C, G, C15]. Also note ASI's finding that text-only workflow memory does not reliably cut steps (AWM 5.9 vs vanilla 5.6): the "text recipes" ablation is expected to show little efficiency gain, which strengthens the case for executable recipes.

**(b) Speculative execution of predictable UI transitions — done at the tool level; open for stateful live web UIs.** Done: Speculative Actions, PASTE, SMC, Speculate with Memory, AOSpec, DSP, ISP; snapshot/fork substrates (TClone, DeltaBox, AOSpec's CoW forks, SMC's draft state); transactional substrates (Atomix, Cordon) [C, G; E113–E128]. Open: speculation in a live, proprietary, non-forkable SaaS app where server state cannot be snapshotted. TClone closes external connections at fork time, DeltaBox has "no network I/O rollback", and AOSpec excludes non-filesystem effects, so none of the substrates reaches the portal's server (E121–E123). Speculation there requires (i) restricting it to state-free transitions (navigation, opening modals, pre-rendering, text entry), (ii) speculating the *observation* (predict the next page so the next model call starts early; AOSpec's observation model hits 33.3% exact on terminal tasks), or (iii) staging effects behind a commit barrier where the portal offers one (Cordon/Atomix style; for Concur plausibly the unsubmitted draft report, inference), with (iv) explicit compensating actions only as the weakest fallback: Saga-style compensation leaked 80% of irreversible effects in Atomix's test, and rollback that erases the agent's effect record enables duplicate effects (E113, E136). Two refinements from v3. (a) "Read-only" is not "side-effect-free" on a SaaS portal: abandoned speculative requests are visible to the vendor at issue time and add load (Ghost Tool Calls: read-only leaks as much intent as naive speculation; ≈3.9× provider calls, calc.) (E129, E130). The classification therefore needs an observability/budget axis next to the Idempotent/Reversible/Compensable/Irreversible axis (E135). (b) A web-action reversibility classifier exists (WebOperator: GET vs POST/PUT/DELETE/PATCH post-execution and button vs link pre-execution, on WebArena), but its pre-execution flag is only ≈37% precise and it serves tree search, not latency speculation (E133; D104). **So no paper classifies web UI transitions by reversibility *and* reports speculation hit rate, accuracy impact and side-effect incidents on a live portal.** This remains the most defensible systems contribution and the most likely to be scooped [C, G]. Speculative Actions proves a **50% latency-reduction cap for single-step speculation**, so the p≈1.0 transitions in Concur should push single-step hit rates toward that cap, and *multi-step* (macro-commit-style) speculation is required to exceed it. SMC's own audit shows why a macro needs guards: a mined-library match alone reproduces the exact future steps in only 34.6% of events (E124). On writes, systems differ (D103): SPORK never speculates writes; Speculative Actions, PASTE, SMC and AOSpec speculate writes only when they are sandboxed/forkable, reversible, dry-run/staged or whitelisted; none speculates an irreversible write against a live external service. "No paper reports speculative or multi-action execution on a live OSWorld desktop with controlled accuracy measurement" [C15] still holds for speculation, with the caveat in D46 that UFO2 and OSWorld 2.0 report multi-action *batching* results.

**(c) Observation pruning, prefix caching, small-model routing — done.** AgentOccam, FocusAgent, MFS, Agentic Compilation's sanitizer; Skim's small-model extraction; RouteLLM/BoPO/WebRouter; provider prompt caching. Novelty ≈ zero; the only publishable angle is the **interaction**: pruning invalidates caches (AgentDiet notes it), routing interacts with recipe coverage — quantify the interactions rather than the levers [C, G].

**(d) Accuracy–latency–cost frontiers over repeated runs — partially done.** AI Agents That Matter and HAL do 2-D accuracy–$ frontiers; τ-bench introduced pass^k; SpeedRunner reports ±1 s.d. over 3 seeds. No work reports **three-way** accuracy–latency–cost frontiers with **field-level** accuracy for form-filling agents over repeated runs, or attributes accuracy loss to each lever [C].

**The combination and the setting.** The integrated stack on an enterprise portal is not published as a unit; no LLM-agent paper evaluates on SAP Concur or expense reporting with policy-derived keys (closest: TheAgentCompany finance/admin tasks; WorkArena/++ on ServiceNow). Reviewers value the answer-key methodology more than the brand [C].

## 5.5 Baselines, ablations, objections (merged)

**Baselines reviewers will demand.** (1) Vanilla ReAct / Browser-Use / BrowserGym GenericAgent on the same model (the baseline of Agent JIT, Skim, FocusAgent). (2) AWM (text workflows) and ASI or SkillWeaver (programmatic skills as tools); SpeedRunner optional. (3) Pure RPA/scripted replay from demos with no LLM (3a); plus the shipped fallback patterns, which are not all naive: (3b) per-step replay with LLM fallback when the element is not found (Hyperbrowser HyperAgent: XPath, 3 retries, then LLM with the cached instruction; UiPath Healing Agent / Power Automate self-healing are the enterprise equivalents); (3c) a replay cache guarded *before* each step by a page-fingerprint/DOM-hash check, with re-inference and cache refresh on drift (Browserbase Stagehand); optionally (3d) whole-task agent re-run and cache regeneration on any unexpected page (Skyvern code caching). This is the most dangerous baseline: if a script alone reaches 95% field accuracy at near-zero cost, the contribution is only the residual. Report heal rate, silent mis-heal rate (field errors after a "successful" heal) and $/successful report for each, since no vendor publishes these (follow-up 3, E160). (4) Skim-style fast path with verifier and warm-started fallback. (5) Speculative Actions and SMC-style macro commit on equal hardware (SMC reports gains relative to SA; do the same). (6) APC plan caching. (7) Prompt-caching-only, small-model-only, cascade/router-only (FrugalGPT/RouteLLM), each alone. (8) Agent JIT Compilation if code is available, else a re-implementation of its planner. (9) Inference-optimized stateless baseline on SGLang/vLLM APC to isolate infrastructure gains. (10) PANDO and SkillDroid adaptations [C, G]. Added in v2 [C15]: (11) **an API-first / hybrid API+GUI agent** (AXIS-, ComputerRL- or UFO2-style) wherever Concur exposes an API — if it solves most reports, the GUI stack is only for the API-less remainder; (12) **EchoPath-style guarded trajectory replay** with lifecycle management, the closest published recipe system; (13) a **budget-shown ReAct / BATS-style** baseline, since showing the agent its remaining budget alone cut cost 31% at equal accuracy.

**Ablations.** Leave-one-out for (a), (b), pruning, caching, routing; pairwise interactions (caching × pruning; recipes × routing); recipe source (own runs only / demos only / both; 0, 1, 3, 5 demos; number of own runs); fallback-trigger threshold sweep (produces the frontier itself); speculation depth/breadth and reversible-only vs all transitions with hit rate, wasted calls and side-effect incidents; pruning aggressiveness vs field accuracy; N ≥ 5 repeated runs with pass^k and bootstrap CIs on every frontier point; distribution shift (synthetic DOM perturbations, a real Concur release, new policy rules and expense types) with recipe breakage and recovery cost; SpeedRunner-style cost-per-report curves vs number of prior reports including amortized compilation; w/o speculative UI execution (quantifies the 71% model-wait that faster LLMs alone cannot remove) [C, G].

**Objections and pre-emptions.**

| Objection | Pre-emption |
|---|---|
| Single-domain testbed / SAP Concur overfitting | Add WorkArena/WorkArena++, WebArena (or WebArena-Verified Hard subset), τ²-bench (AppWorld as alternative). Measure UI-transition entropy in Concur vs open-web benchmarks; show graceful degradation to exploratory execution when entropy is high. Show the 57% page-determined, p≈1.0 and 71% model-wait statistics generalize (Skim's 66.7% navigational steps is an external anchor) |
| Live proprietary portal is not reproducible | Release a Concur-like simulator or offline record-and-replay artifact (Mind2Web-style), anonymized policies and keys; put headline claims on public benchmarks and position Concur as the case study; report portal version and dates |
| It's engineering, not research | Contribute (i) a formal model of expected speedup vs recipe coverage, guard precision and speculation hit rate (Speculative Actions' cost–latency analysis is a template); (ii) safety semantics for speculation without snapshots (two-axis classification of UI transitions by state effect and vendor observability; commit barrier before compensation; measured classifier precision and incident audit), which no prior system provides for a live SaaS back end (TClone, DeltaBox, AOSpec and SMC all need forkable state; Cordon and Atomix were not evaluated on a GUI portal); (iii) a measurement methodology (field-level, 3-D frontiers) |
| Fragility of speculation; non-Markovian rollbacks | A fixed per-transition classification on two axes. **State effect** (Idempotent / Reversible / Compensable / Irreversible, per Revisable by Design): navigation, pagination and text entry into unsaved fields may be speculated; saves of draft lines only behind a commit barrier or with a tested compensation; POST submissions, approvals and final submissions never. **Observability/load**: every speculative request the vendor sees is logged and rate-counted, even when read-only (Ghost Tool Calls). Report per class: hit rate, wasted requests per report, rollback/compensation count, and side-effect incidents, audited Atomix/PASTE-style (effects detected vs prevented from committing; final-state divergence vs a non-speculative run). Use a gate and not compensation for anything irreversible (Atomix: Saga-compensation leaked 80% of irreversible effects vs 0% gated). Keep a durable effect log outside the agent so rollback cannot erase a released effect (Safe to Resume). Measure the classifier's precision, which is the weak link (WebOperator's pre-execution flag: ≈37%) [E113, E129, E133, E135, E136] |
| "Speculative reads leak intent and load the vendor's servers" (Ghost Tool Calls, Jun 2026) | Count and report speculative requests reaching the portal per report (wasted requests), with and without speculation. Prefer speculating the *observation* on the client (predict the page and start the model call) over issuing the request early. Where a request must be issued early, limit it to pages the non-speculative run will almost surely visit (p≈1.0 transitions), so abandoned ("ghost") requests stay near zero; report the ghost-request rate next to the hit rate (Ghost Tool Calls measured 82–89% abandoned candidates for an LLM planner) [E129, E130] |
| Answer-key leakage into recipe learning | Learn recipes only from trajectories and demos; keys only for evaluation; disjoint report sets; report results when recipe learning uses only self-judged success |
| Comparison fairness (caching, hardware, provider latency) | Report $ with and without caching (HAL reports without); fix region, time of day, model version; report API latency distributions; give speculative baselines equal compute; include a non-predictive concurrency control (same extra parallelism, no prediction) so the speed-up attributed to speculation is not parallelism the serial baseline left unused, and report speed-up versus server load/rate-limit headroom (objection raised by a secondary, agent-authored simulation, argszero/silicon-science-cs #50, E138, and by 2606.07846's own limitations, E131) |
| Lossy acceleration hides errors | Report per-field error deltas and worst-case field; include an abstain/escalate-to-human option in the frontier |
| **"Why click through Concur at all? Use the API."** (raised by AXIS, ComputerRL, UFO2, Beyond Browsing — added in v2 [C15]) | Measure and report the workflow's API coverage (which fields/steps have a usable, permitted API; which policy checks live only in the UI); include an API-first baseline where an API exists; position GUI recipes + speculation as the method for the API-less, permission-gated remainder that most enterprise portals still have; cite AXIS's like-for-like agent comparison (API-first vs UI agent on Word: ≈2× faster, 3.2→2.0 steps, cost halved, 52→84% success) as what API access buys; its −65–70% is against manual human operation |
| **"Guarded replay with lifecycle already exists" (EchoPath, Sep 2026)** | Compare directly on OSWorld-Verified repeat tasks; differentiate on demo mixing, speculation, field-level error attribution, write-heavy multi-page forms, and replay success measured across UI versions over time — none of which EchoPath reports; note EchoPath's baseline is another memory system (Synapse, 91.8% vs EchoPath-Codex 91.2%), not a no-memory agent; its gain is cost/time at equal success; its code link was 404 on 2026-09-27 |

## 5.6 Framings

1. **Profile-guided tiered compilation for agents (JIT with de-optimization).** Interpret with the LLM; detect hot, stable trajectory fragments; compile them into guarded recipes; de-optimize to the LLM on guard failure, resuming from the current state. Venue: MLSys (OSDI/SOSP if the runtime is substantial). Position against Agent JIT (compiles from the instruction, not profiles), Agentic Compilation (no guards/profiling), SpeedRunner/ASI/SkillDroid (skills as LLM-called tools or mobile-only), APC. Distinguishing claim: profile-guided + guard-based de-optimization with measured coverage and mis-speculation cost [C, G].
2. **Branch prediction for stateful UIs: speculative execution without snapshots.** Treat UI transitions as branches; predict and execute (or pre-fetch the observation of) near-deterministic transitions; classify by reversibility with commit and compensation rules. Venue: MLSys or NeurIPS. Position against Speculative Actions (general, lossless), SMC/TClone (need snapshots), PASTE (tools), Speculate with Memory (transition tables), Skim (read-only). Distinguishing claim: safe speculation in live, irreversible web apps with hit-rate and side-effect analysis [C, G].
3. **Where agent time and money go: field-level, three-way Pareto accounting for enterprise automation.** A measurement paper on answer-key-graded workflows decomposing the 13-minute, 100-call run into page-determined vs reasoning steps and reporting per-lever accuracy loss with repeated runs and consistency metrics. Venue: NeurIPS D&B or ICLR; strongest with a released Concur-like environment. Position against AI Agents That Matter, HAL, Efficient Agents (2-D), τ-bench pass^k, WorkArena++ [C]. Gemini's "microeconomics of agentic automation" is the same framing [G].

Recommendation (both providers): lead with 1 + 2 as one MLSys submission and use 3's methodology as the evaluation backbone; 3 alone risks reading as a case study unless an environment is released.

## 5.7 Public benchmarks and cost of a full ablation grid (reconciled)

| Benchmark | Why it fits | Size / steps | Cost per run (reported → extrapolated) | Grid (~9 configs × 5 repeats = 45 runs) |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **WorkArena L1 + WorkArena++ L2/L3** (ServiceNow; NeurIPS 2024 D&B) | Enterprise forms/lists with human-coded oracles; closest public analogue to Concur; free developer instances; human success 93.9%; GPT-4o 2.1–43% depending on level | ++ has 682 tasks (235 each L2/L3 in the standard curriculum), 50-step limits, up to ~140 oracle actions; L1 eval = 33 tasks × 10 seeds = 330 episodes | **Measured:** L1 $55.6 per 330-episode run (GPT-4.1 GenericAgent, 53.6%); BrowserGym Claude 3.5 Sonnet: L1 $0.303/task, L2 $1.274/task, L3 $0.815/task. Extrapolated: L2+L3 ≈$250–300 per run at GPT-4.1-class prices | L1 ≈$2.5k; L2+L3 ≈$11–14k; 5–10× less with small models. **Gemini's "$15–50 per full run" is inconsistent with the measured $55.6 for L1 alone — do not use** |
| **WebArena** (812 tasks; or WebArena-Verified Hard 258) | Where AWM, ASI, SkillWeaver, AgentOccam, FocusAgent report; self-hosted Docker | 30-step limit (BrowserGym) | **Measured:** $59.0 on a 381-task subset (GPT-4.1, 36.5%); BrowserGym Claude 3.5 Sonnet $0.171/task. Extrapolated ≈$125 per full run | ≈$2.7k (381 subset) / ≈$5.6k (full); Hard-258 subset cuts ≈83% of tasks while keeping the difficult ones [G]. Gemini's "$50–100 per full run" is plausible only for cheap models |
| **τ²-bench** (50 airline / 115 retail / 114 telecom) | Policy-constrained multi-turn workflows mirroring policy-derived keys; native pass^k; SMC and HAL comparators | Dual-control | **Measured:** ~$40 per trial, all domains, gpt-4.1; HAL τ-Airline $0.31 (Gemini 2.0 Flash, 28%) to $180.49 (Opus 4.1, 54%); o4-mini High $11.36 at 56% | ≈$1.8k at gpt-4.1 prices; $7k+ with 4 trials/task |
| Alternatives: **AppWorld** (750 tasks, 457 APIs; GPT-4o ~49% normal / ~30% challenge; state-based collateral-damage tests; SMC comparator; ~$0.02–0.03/task with a small model, unverified); **TheAgentCompany** (175 tasks; ~$735 per run at $4.20/task — subset only); **OSWorld/OSWorld-Verified/OSWorld-Pro** (desktop; supplementary if visual SoM matters; ~$10–20 VM per run before vision-model API costs) | | | | [C, G] |

## 5.8 Recommendations (merged)

1. Re-run the novelty search every 2–4 weeks; speculative agent execution had ≥8 arXiv submissions in mid-2026; SMC (Sep 2026) is the most dangerous overlap for the speculation component and **EchoPath (Sep 2026) for the recipe component** [C, C15].
1b. Before building, audit Concur's API surface and state the API-coverage number in the paper; reviewers will ask (§5.5) [C15].
2. Make pure-RPA-replay and Skim/Agent-JIT baselines central; if scripts alone solve most reports, say so and claim the residual [C].
3. Scope speculation to what is safe without snapshots (observation pre-fetch; reversible navigation); report side-effect incidents as a metric [C, G].
4. Publish the characterization statistics (57% page-determined, p≈1.0 transitions, 71% model wait) on public benchmarks, and add the UI-transition-entropy measurement [C, G].
5. Report field-level accuracy loss per lever with CIs over N ≥ 5 runs and 3-D frontiers; include cached vs uncached $ [C].
6. Validate on WorkArena++ first for reproducibility; keep Concur as the real-world case study [G, C].

| D95 | §1.8 Regime A dominant term (E22–E25) [followup-1] | §1.8 A says the dominant term is "Prefill of a growing multimodal context; number of sequential model calls" and "screenshots + actions <3.5%". Restate A's dominant term as "LLM time **for multi-call planner/judge scaffolds**; environment waits can dominate single-call agents". |

The "900 ms" baseline and the "5 ms" rollback were not found in v2 (E122) | Cite v2: checkpoint ≈10.83 ms, rollback ≈1.86 ms, 64-way fork p99 14.74 ms. Replace the alphaXiv link with https://arxiv.org/html/2605.22781v2. The older numbers are possibly v1 (version drift), but v1 was not read → still-open. Add "no network I/O rollback" to the mechanism column |
| D103 | "Every speculative system falls back to serial execution for writes" [followup-2] | Dossier §5.4(b) (from C15): "every speculative system in doc 15 (SPORK, Speculative Actions, PASTE, DSP) falls back to serial execution for writes". Primary sources: true for **SPORK** (E128: writes "always take the serial fallback path"). **Speculative Actions** allows "idempotent, reversible, or sandboxed speculative side effects" and its OS extension applies live provisional mutations (E126). **PASTE v3** speculates side-effecting tools that have an "explicit safe-speculation policy or a non-mutating transformed execution, such as dry-run execution or execution against staging resources" (E127). **SMC** replays forkable AppWorld mutations and rejects irreversible/unknown ones by API-name rules (E125). **AOSpec** speculates side-effecting tools inside CoW filesystem forks (E123). DSP not re-checked | **C15 overstated.** Reword as: SPORK speculates only read-only tools. The others speculate writes only when they are sandboxed/forkable, reversible, dry-run/staged, or whitelisted. None speculates an irreversible write against a live external service. Patch P11 |
| D104 | "No paper classifies web UI transitions by reversibility" [followup-2] | Dossier §5.4(b): "no paper classifies web UI transitions by reversibility and reports hit rate and accuracy impact in such a portal". WebOperator (Dec 2025) classifies web actions as safe/destructive with pre-execution DOM heuristics and post-execution HTTP-method heuristics on WebArena, and reports ≈37% pre-execution precision (E133). Atomix assigns effect classes to tool adapters and runs on WebArena/OSWorld (E114/E116) | The **classification** half of the claim is false as worded. The full conjunction (classification + speculation hit rate + accuracy impact + live proprietary portal) still has no paper. Reword; cite WebOperator as prior art for the classifier and its low precision as motivation. Patch P11 |
| D105 | Cordon as a "compensation substrate without snapshots" [followup-2] | Dossier §3.3 Cordon row: "a candidate commit/compensation substrate for speculation without snapshots". Cordon (E120) *does* snapshot local state ("shadow state", rollback to "pre-transaction anchors"). It does **not** compensate external effects; it **stages** them in an outbox until validation or approval. Its evaluation has no web/GUI tasks, and its wall-clock includes human approval wait (E117–E119) | Reword: Cordon is a *commit-barrier (staging)* substrate. It is applicable to a portal only where the UI itself offers a staging area (e.g. an unsubmitted draft; inference, unverified for Concur). It gives no compensation for released effects. Patch P2 |
| D106 | "Read-only / navigation speculation is safe" [followup-2] | Dossier §5.4(b)(i) and the §5.5 row "Fragility of speculation; non-Markovian rollbacks" treat navigation and pagination (read-only transitions) as safe to speculate. The same assumption appears in SPORK, Speculate with Memory's read-only whitelist and 2606.07846 condition (a). Ghost Tool Calls (E129): a read-only restriction (SIA) leaks intent exactly as much as unrestricted speculation (0.97 vs 0.97; AgentDojo 0.750 vs 0.750), because the call is visible to the provider at issue time. Naive speculation also sends ≈3.9× the provider calls (E130, calc.) | Refine, do not reverse. Read-only speculation is safe for *state* but not for *observability* or *load*. On a SaaS portal a speculative GET is logged by the vendor, may count against rate limits, and may trigger view-side effects such as "viewed" or "opened" marks and audit entries (inference; unverified for Concur). Add issue-time observability and request budget to the safety classification. Patches P11, P12 |
| D107 | Compensation as a viable third path [followup-2] | Dossier §5.4(b)(iii) lists "explicit compensating actions" alongside (i)/(ii) without a caveat. Atomix Table 2 (E113): Saga-style compensation leaked 400/500 (80%) of irreversible effects; checkpoint-replay leaked 40%. Safe to Resume (E136): both commit orderings fail on 90/96 and 93/96 workflows without durable effect records. RAC (E137) fails open when no compensation is found | Keep (iii) but mark it as the weakest option. Compensation is safe only for effects the portal can semantically undo (K class, E135), needs a durable effect log outside the agent, and never covers irreversible (X) actions. Patch P11 |

# PART VII — Provider scorecard (取长补短)

| Dimension | Claude [C] | ChatGPT [O] | Gemini [G] |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Source hygiene** | Consistent primary/secondary labeling; flags paper-internal inconsistencies (OSWorld-Human $2.43; Efficient Agents; τ-bench); exact table extraction (OSWorld-Human Table 1) | Best: measurement-definition discipline (calls ≠ steps ≠ turns; inference ≠ wall-clock; attempt ≠ success); version-aware (uses revised tables: FocusAgent Aug 2026, Continuum v7, PASTE v3, Agentix NSDI); labels every derived number as "calculated"; explicitly says what was not retrieved | Weakest: cites trackers, blogs, a Web3 Medium post, a YouTube video, an awesome-list, a personal homepage; cites an AI-generated idea file as a paper (MacroPO); repeats uncorrected abstracts (Efficient Agents); mis-states APC and Speculate-with-Memory; misattributes OSWorld 2.0's finding; stale/inconsistent pricing; Google-Docs export lost formulas to images |
| **Unique, valuable finds** | HAL per-task cost table; OSWorld-Human observation-cost (3–26 s AXTree) and 4–12 calls/judge; Google 3.2Q tokens; Microsoft Foundry 128k downgrade; unified Sept-2026 price table; power calculation; the 2026 novelty landscape (Agent JIT, Skim, SMC, SpeedRunner, AAPT, LOOP, Activity Frames, Hyperbrowser); UI-CUBE; Efficient Benchmarking (Ndzomga); DeltaSelect. **P3 run (doc 15, v2):** ComputerRL, AXIS, Beyond Browsing, ECLAIR, EchoPath, AgentReuse, ToolCaching, KVCOMM, DroidSpeak, LLM-Tool Compiler, ParaGUI, ScreenSeekeR, Overthinking (Cuadron), BATS, TTI; Speculative Actions' 50% single-step cap; ASI's AWM-uses-more-steps re-run; vendor effort numbers (Opus 4.5 −76% tokens, GPT-5 −45% tool calls); warming $2.45 vs LATS $134.50; venue confirmations (WALT ICLR 2026 poster, AI Agents That Matter TMLR 2025, DSP ICLR 2026 proceedings, Prune4Web AAAI 2026); the OSWorld-Human MLSys camera-ready PDF; the clearest "lossless vs lossy" classification per family | BrowserGym per-task timings for WebArena/WorkArena; original OSWorld/WebArena human stopwatch times; Online-Mind2Web Operator 44 min / 2.6×; OpenRouter 2026 telemetry (15×, Feb crossover, 14×, 70% cached); Anthropic 4×/15×; OpenAI Codex 63.3%; Gartner primary; Temporal survey; "Why Johnny Can't Use Agents"; Agentic Test-Time Scaling (9.6×); Webwright; Claude in Chrome → Haiku default; Anthropic CU best practices; Agentic Coding in the Wild; AWO "fewer calls ≠ faster"; StepWise; Fara-7B; AQuaUI; TRACE; GoClick; PreAct overhead; binomial bound; correction of the Browser Use "task time" | Mind2Web 2 human timing (8.4 vs 18.4 min); HUMAN Security 7,851%; Cost of Dynamic Reasoning energy figures; EY $0.04→$1.20; Gartner Aug-2026 5× workflow-cost release; Gartner Apr-2026 ROI survey; Operator timeline; the broad 2026 systems sweep (TokenPilot, PEEK, SpecBox, DeltaBox, CacheScout, Helium, AsymCache, AgentKVShift, DualPath, Atomix, Spork, Cordon, AgentServe); SkillDroid, PANDO, MFS/GEPA, Prune4Web, SWE-Pruner, Qwen-UI-Agent, CUA-Universe; Odysseys, Reliability Science Framework (VAF), best@k/avg@k; "environment 53.7%" and "tools 35–61%" latency shares; business/TCO framing |
| **Errors / weaknesses** | Mislabeled Browser Use inference time as task time (D1); imprecise on OSWorld 2.0 human timing (D2); internal v1/v2 inconsistency across its own reports (D39); missed BrowserGym timings, the OpenRouter 2026 telemetry, the HCI study and the controlled TTS experiment. **P3 run (doc 15):** uses FocusAgent v1 and AppAgentX v1 numbers (D44, D48); repeats Efficient Agents' abstract figures (D9); claims no controlled multi-action study exists on OSWorld (D46); leaves Autellix's venue unverified although NSDI 2026 exists (D52); its Ares WebArena numbers are self-flagged automated extraction (D49); and it explicitly did not check OSWorld 2.0 or TheAgentCompany — the two benchmarks that carry Part I | Two survey runs disagree on FocusAgent (D15) and quote DSP against different baselines; terse to the point of density; no novelty run in the set; some Speculate-with-Memory nuance (1.05×) may be over-conservative relative to the paper's own estimates | Listed above; also benchmark cost estimates too low (WorkArena++ "$15–50"), "median" for a mean (D3), "88%" (D19), Gemini 2.5 Flash TTFT via secondary, EY/Uber/TCO figures unverified, off-topic robotics papers in the survey |
| **Best use of each** | Backbone for evidence tables, pricing, novelty positioning, and statistical protocol | Backbone for definitions, corrections, budget sensitivity, and the "what papers actually report" audit | Idea generator for breadth (2026 systems papers, business framing) — every item must be re-verified before citation |

Which document to open for what: evidence-with-caveats → doc 14 (O) then doc 8 (C); mapping table → doc 1 (O) and doc 7 (C); landscape tables → docs 2 and 3 (O) and doc 15 (C, best lossless/lossy classification and 2026 systems coverage) with doc 13 (G) as a lead list; protocol and budget → docs 4 (O) and 5 (C); novelty → doc 6 (C) with doc 11 (G) for SkillDroid/PANDO/MFS; business framing → doc 12 (G) with verification.

# PART VIII — Gaps, verification queue, and suggested next investigations

## 8.1 Evidence nobody found (true gaps)

- Side-by-side **agent and human wall-clock on identical tasks** on any standard computer-use/web benchmark (OSWorld, OSWorld-Verified, OSWorld 2.0, WebArena, WorkArena/++). Human times exist on each of these but with mismatched samples or no agent wall-clock (OSWorld §3.4 and WebArena timed, method NR, E12/E16; OSWorld 2.0 annotator estimate with no agent wall-clock, E5/E9; OSWorld-Human author estimate, E1; WorkArena++ recorded but unpublished, E16; Mind2Web 2 cross-sample, E14). Same-task pairs exist only at small n or outside GUI benchmarks: AXIS user study (5 Word tasks, 20 people, instrumented: a 2024 GUI agent 1.69× slower than manual on easy tasks, 0.93× on hard, calc.), CADWorld (6 tasks, best case 1.5–4.3× slower), FrontierFinance (10 tasks, agents 19–26× faster at lower quality), GDPval (220 tasks, self-reported estimate vs API time) (§1.1a; D119). Cheapest route to close it: time one agent on UI-CUBE (human seconds on all 226 tasks, E15), or obtain per-task human times for OSWorld §3.4 / WebArena §3.2 / METR / APEX-Agents' 96 baselined tasks and time agents on those tasks.
- **First-party agentic token share** from OpenAI/Anthropic/Microsoft (only Codex output-token shares and Anthropic's 4×/15×); analyst token-share reports (Morgan Stanley, Goldman Sachs, SemiAnalysis, Epoch AI, Artificial Analysis).
- Per-task **timing/call/token datasets for Claude computer use, Claude in Chrome, Operator/ChatGPT agent, ChatGPT Work cloud browser**.
- **Quantified HCI wait-tolerance** for computer-use agents (only the qualitative Johnny study).
- Consulting-firm **latency-blocker percentages** (McKinsey, BCG, Bain, Deloitte, KPMG, PwC).
- Primary **Groq/Cerebras/SambaNova** agent-speed claims; **independent** Browserbase speed data (vendor claims exist: E154).
- **Measured heal/replay success, heal latency and cost per successful run for any shipped RPA-with-LLM-fallback product** (UiPath, Power Automate, Automation Anywhere, Stagehand, Skyvern, HyperAgent publish none — follow-up 3, E160).
- **Independent** end-to-end agent timing on fast hardware or fast tiers. Vendor claims now exist (NVIDIA-Groq, Cerebras, SambaNova, Browserbase, OpenAI Ultrafast; E87, followup-6), but the only end-to-end computer-use timing is Cerebras's vendor-run single task (E201), and no third party has measured Anthropic or OpenAI fast tiers (E209).
- A standard **accounting unit** (calls per solved task + prefill tokens + turns + tool time).
- **Replay success across UI versions over time** for any skill/recipe/cache system, and any study of **cache poisoning** in shared agent caches (added in v2 from doc 15).
- **Any serving-system evaluation on a GUI/computer-use workload** (re-checked 2026-09-27 against the primary papers for 15 systems in followup-5 — CacheScout, Helium, TokenPilot, PEEK, SpecBox, DeltaBox, AsymCache, AgentKVShift, DualPath, UNISON, PBKV, AgentServe, Continuum v7, Agentix, ThunderAgent: none uses a GUI, browser, OSWorld or WebArena workload).
- **A whole-stack end-to-end measurement** (lossless serving + call-count reducers + lossy levers) on a computer-use benchmark with dollars and wall-clock — the opening the Part V plan can take.

## 8.3 Suggested follow-up deep-research prompts (for the next round)

1. **Latency decomposition by architecture** — "For each of: screenshot-based CUAs (OSWorld/OSWorld-Verified agents), AXTree/DOM web agents (BrowserGym, Browser Use, Skim), and cached coding agents (Claude Code, Codex, Copilot), find every published per-step latency breakdown (model prefill/decode, screenshot/AXTree generation, browser/tool execution, queueing) with the harness, model, caching state and date; produce a table that states which term dominates and why."
2. **Speculation safety semantics for stateful web UIs** — "Survey all 2025–2026 work on speculative/asynchronous execution of side-effecting tool or UI actions (Atomix, Cordon, TClone, DeltaBox, SMC, AOSpec, Speculative Actions, PASTE, Spork): what isolation/rollback/compensation mechanisms each assumes, which are feasible on a live SaaS portal without snapshots, and what hit rates and side-effect incidents they report."
3. **Enterprise RPA + LLM fallback products** — "Document what UiPath, Automation Anywhere, Microsoft Power Automate, Hyperbrowser, Browserbase, Browser Use, LOOP and similar ship for record/replay with LLM fallback: failure detection, selector healing, measured replay success, cost per run, and any published latency numbers."
4. **Human-time baselines** — "Find every benchmark that reports timed human completion (OSWorld §3.4, WebArena, Mind2Web 2, OSWorld 2.0, WorkArena++, GDPval, METR time horizons, APEX-Agents, Agents' Last Exam) with the timing method, sample and task overlap with agent runs; identify where a clean agent-vs-human wall-clock ratio can be computed."
5. **2026 agent-serving papers** — "Verify and tabulate CacheScout, Helium, TokenPilot, PEEK, SpecBox, DeltaBox, AsymCache, AgentKVShift, DualPath, UNISON, PBKV, AgentServe, Continuum v7, Agentix: baseline, hardware, workload, headline gain, quality reporting, and whether code is released."
6. **Vendor speed tiers and computer-use latency** — "Collect primary documentation for every latency-oriented tier or product move since Oct 2025 (Anthropic fast mode and Claude in Chrome defaults; OpenAI Fast/Flex; Microsoft Foundry priority; Google Flash/Flash-Lite computer use; Groq, Cerebras, SambaNova agent claims; Browserbase/Browser Use) with prices, measured tokens/s, TTFT and any end-to-end computer-use timing."

# Appendix B — Glossary of measurement terms used in this dossier

- **Step / turn**: one observe→decide→act cycle against the environment (OSWorld 2.0 uses "steps" in Table 3 and "turns" in the text; batched tool calls put several actions in one step).
- **Tool call**: one action or tool invocation; several per step when batched.
- **Model call**: one LLM request; several per step in planner/judge/reflection scaffolds (GTA1: 4–12 planning calls per judging call).
- **Critical-path depth**: the longest chain of dependent model/tool calls; governs latency under parallel execution.
- **Wall-clock**: request accepted → final committed state, including environment time, tool time, queueing and rate-limit waits.
- **Inference time**: model-only decision time; excludes browser/tool/environment time (Browser Use's 68 s vs 225–330 s).
- **Prefill / decode**: input-processing vs token-generation phases; prefill-bound when uncached contexts grow (OSWorld-Human), decode-bound when caches hold (2605.26297).
- **Cost per attempt / per success / cost-of-pass**: mean $ per attempted task; total $ ÷ successes (pooled); taskwise E[C_i]/p_i.
- **pass^k / pass@k**: probability all k trials succeed / any of k succeeds.
- **WES**: Weighted Efficiency Score — success weighted by step efficiency vs human reference (OSWorld-Human).
- **Lossless (sense 1/2/3)**: by-construction trajectory preservation / statistical non-inferiority within δ / no observed degradation.
- **Regime A/B/C**: latency profiles for screenshot multi-call agents / AXTree single-call web agents / cached coding agents (§1.8).

*End of dossier.*
